        Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

## Notes
- Reuse the same Anthropic client setup from Plan 3.
- Cost: each outbound now incurs an extra Sonnet call (the OOB check). Make sure `record_llm_cost` is called for these.
- Both the OOB check and scoring are good candidates for prompt caching when called repeatedly — cache the static parts (anchors, instructions).

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity.         Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

## Notes
- Reuse the same Anthropic client setup from Plan 3.
- Cost: each outbound now incurs an extra Sonnet call (the OOB check). Make sure `record_llm_cost` is called for these.
- Both the OOB check and scoring are good candidates for prompt caching when called repeatedly — cache the static parts (anchors, instructions).

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the new `hooks.check_oob` default against existing tests. `tests/test_send_outbound.py` has an autouse fixture that sets the hook to `none`, so preserving an intentional no-hook test path is compatible with the plan's module-level default, but the new plan 3 e2e test must install a counting hook if it wants to assert the pass-through was invoked. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery: recovery: re-queueing crashed turns with multiple `triggering_message_ids` is underspecified because the coalescer only exposes single-message `add`, not a way to restore a whole burst atomically. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery-query: recovery query: the plan's claimed gin-backed lookup uses `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)`, which is unlikely to use the array gin index as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: media processing state after success: the success criteria for text and outbound mention processed state, but revised steps 11 and 12 do not explicitly say successful voice or image messages should remain `raw` for the agentic loop rather than be marked `processed` by the media layer. this is likely intentional because plan 3 owns loop processing, but it is a contract point worth keeping visible so the executor does not mark media messages processed before the agent sees them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the scope of the two-phase split against the five-primitive write tools. the split solves ordering, but it broadens orchestration complexity because phase b must reason over phase a's transcript and the sent outbound without read tools; this is a new design risk specifically for dedup and multi-primitive decisions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] two-phase-dedup-tension: two-phase dedup tension: phase b is write-only, so the agent cannot perform the required search-before-write reads if it enters the write phase without enough context to choose add vs update vs supersede. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

## Notes
- Reuse the same Anthropic client setup from Plan 3.
- Cost: each outbound now incurs an extra Sonnet call (the OOB check). Make sure `record_llm_cost` is called for these.
- Both the OOB check and scoring are good candidates for prompt caching when called repeatedly — cache the static parts (anchors, instructions).

return int(_attr(usage, field, 0) or 0)\n+\n+\n+async def _record_response_cost(pool: Any, usage: Any) -> None:\n+    settings = get_settings()\n+    input_price = settings.anthropic_input_usd_per_mtok\n+    output_price = settings.anthropic_output_usd_per_mtok\n+    input_tokens = _usage_tokens(usage, \"input_tokens\")\n+    cache_create = _usage_tokens(usage, \"cache_creation_input_tokens\")\n+    cache_read = _usage_tokens(usage, \"cache_read_input_tokens\")\n+    output_tokens = _usage_tokens(usage, \"output_tokens\")\n+    regular_input_tokens = max(0, input_tokens - cache_create - cache_read)\n+    dollars = (\n+        regular_input_tokens * input_price\n+        + cache_create * input_price * 1.25\n+        + cache_read * input_price * 0.10\n+        + output_tokens * output_price\n+    ) / 1_000_000\n+    if dollars > 0:\n+        await record_llm_cost(pool, \"text\", dollars)\n+\n+\n+def _response_text(response: Any) -> str:\n+    parts: response = await client.messages.create(\n+                model=settings.conversational_model,\n+                max_tokens=600,\n+                system=[{\"type\": \"text\", \"text\": _STATIC_INSTRUCTIONS, \"cache_control\": {\"type\": \"ephemeral\"}}],\n+                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": json.dumps(payload, default=str)}],\n+            )\n+        await _record_response_cost(pool, _attr(response, \"usage\", {}))\n+        result = _parse_checker_output(_response_text(response))\n+    except Exception as exc:\n+        return _failure_result(entries, exc)\n+\n+    if result.checker_failed:\n+        return _failure_result(entries, RuntimeError(result.reason))\n+    return result\n*** Add File: app/services/scoring.py\n+\"\"\"Observation significance scoring helpers.\"\"\"\n+\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import asyncio\n+import json\n+import logging\n+from typing import Any, NamedTuple\n+\n+import anthropic\n+\n+from app.config import get_settings\n+from app.services.spend import is_under_cap, record_llm_cost\n+\n+logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n+\n+SCORING_PROMPT_VERSION = \"v1\"\n+FAILED_SCORING_PROMPT_VERSION = \"v1-failed\"\n+DEFAULT_SCORING_MODEL = \"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001\"\n+SCORING_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 30\n+\n+# Defaults for Haiku-family pricing until Plan 4's config cleanup exposes these.\n+HAIKU_INPUT_USD_PER_MTOK = 1.0\n+HAIKU_OUTPUT_USD_PER_MTOK = 5.0\n+\n+_SCORING_INSTRUCTIONS = \"\"\"Score a relationship observation's significance on a 1-5 scale.\n+\n+Return JSON only with this shape:\n+{\"score\":1,\"reason\":\"short explanation\"}\n+\n+Anchor examples:\n+1 - Trivial. Marginal pattern, weak evidence, low relevance even if true.\n+Example: \"He uses slightly more emojis on weekends.\"\n+2 - Minor. Real but small. Worth recording, not worth surfacing proactively.\n+Example: \"She tends to send longer messages in the morning.\"\n+3 - Notable. Solid pattern with real relevance to how they relate. Worth surfacing when relevant context arises.\n+Example: \"He brings up work frustration before getting sharp with her.\"\n+4 - Significant. Strong pattern materially affecting how the relationship functions. Should actively inform engagement.\n+Example: \"Their conflicts cool faster when she initiates the repair; much slower when he does.\"\n+5 - Core. Defining pattern of the relationship dynamic. Always-on context.\n+Example: \"Long walks have been their primary reconnection mechanism for years; it is how they consistently repair.\"\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+\n+class RescoreReport(NamedTuple):\n+    scanned: int\n+    rescored: int\n+    still_failed: int\n+\n+\n+def _attr(obj: Any, name: str, default: Any = None) -> Any:\n+    if isinstance(obj, dict):\n+        return obj.get(name, default)\n+    return getattr(obj, name, default)\n+\n+\n+def _usage_tokens(usage: Any, field: str) -> int:\n+    return int(_attr(usage, field, 0) or 0)\n+\n+\n+async def _record_response_cost(pool: Any, usage: Any) -> None:\n+    input_tokens = _usage_tokens(usage, \"input_tokens\")\n+    cache_create = _usage_tokens(usage, \"cache_creation_input_tokens\")\n+    cache_read = _usage_tokens(usage, \"cache_read_input_tokens\")\n+    output_tokens = _usage_tokens(usage, \"output_tokens\")\n+    regular_input_tokens = max(0, input_tokens - cache_create - cache_read)\n+    dollars = (\n+        regular_input_tokens * HAIKU_INPUT_USD_PER_MTOK\n+        + cache_create * HAIKU_INPUT_USD_PER_MTOK * 1.25\n+        + cache_read * HAIKU_INPUT_USD_PER_MTOK * 0.10\n+        + output_tokens * HAIKU_OUTPUT_USD_PER_MTOK\n+    ) / 1_000_000\n+    if dollars > 0:\n+        await record_llm_cost(pool, \"text\", dollars)\n+\n+\n+def _response_text(response: Any) -> str:\n+    parts: list[str] = []\n+    for block in _attr(response, \"content\", []) or []:\n+        if _attr(block, \"type\") == \"text\":\n+            parts.append(str(_attr(block, \"text\", \"\")))\n+    return \"\\n\".join(part for part in parts if part).strip()\n+\n+\n+def _failure(reason: str) -> tuple[None, str, str]:\n+    return None, f\"scoring failed: {reason}\", FAILED_SCORING_PROMPT_VERSION\n+\n+\n+def _parse_score(text: str) -> tuple[int, str]:\n+    data = json.loads(text)\n+    score = int(data[\"score\"])\n+    if score < 1 or score > 5:\n+        raise ValueError(f\"score out of range: {score}\")\n+    reason = str(data.get(\"reason\") or \"\").strip()\n+    if not reason:\n+        raise ValueError(\"missing reason\")\n+    return score, reason\n+\n+\n+async def score_observation(\n+    pool: Any,\n+    *,\n+    content: str,\n+    client: Any | None = None,\n+) -> tuple[int | None, str, str]:\n+    \"\"\"Return `(score, reason, prompt_version)` or `(None, reason, 'v1-failed')`.\"\"\"\n+    settings = get_settings()\n+    if client is None:\n+        client = anthropic.AsyncAnthropic([REDACTED]())\n+\n+    try:\n+        if not await is_under_cap(pool, \"text\"):\n+            raise RuntimeError(\"text LLM spend cap exceeded\")\n+        async with asyncio.timeout(SCORING_TIMEOUT_SECONDS):\n+            response = await client.messages.create(\n+                model=DEFAULT_SCORING_MODEL,\n+                max_tokens=300,\n+                system=[{\"type\": \"text\", \"text\": _SCORING_INSTRUCTIONS, \"cache_control\": {\"type\": \"ephemeral\"}}],\n+                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": json.dumps({\"observation\": content})}],\n+            )\n+        await _record_response_cost(pool, _attr(response, \"usage\", {}))\n+        score, reason = _parse_score(_response_text(response))\n+    except Exception as exc:\n+        logger.warning(\"observation scoring failed: %s\", exc)\n+        return _failure(str(exc))\n+    return score, reason, SCORING_PROMPT_VERSION\n+\n+\n+async def rescore_observations(\n+    pool: Any,\n+    *,\n+    prompt_version_threshold: str = SCORING_PROMPT_VERSION,\n+    client: Any | None = None,\n+) -> RescoreReport:\n+    \"\"\"Manually re-score observations with missing, failed, or older prompt versions.\"\"\"\n+    rows = await pool.fetch(\n+        \"\"\"\n+        SELECT id, content\n+        FROM observations\n+        WHERE scoring_prompt_version IS NULL\n+           OR scoring_prompt_version < $1\n+           OR scoring_prompt_version LIKE '%failed'\n+        ORDER BY created_at\n+        \"\"\",\n+        prompt_version_threshold,\n+    )\n+    rescored = 0\n+    still_failed = 0\n+    for row in rows:\n+        score, _reason, prompt_version = await score_observation(pool, content=row[\"content\"], client=client)\n+        await pool.execute(\n+            \"\"\"\n+            UPDATE observations\n+            SET significance = $1,\n+                scoring_prompt_version = $2,\n+                last_reinforced_at = COALESCE(last_reinforced_at, now())\n+            WHERE id = $3\n+            \"\"\",\n+            score,\n+            prompt_version,\n+            row[\"id\"],\n+        )\n+        if score is None:\n+            still_failed += 1\n+        else:\n+            rescored += 1\n+    return RescoreReport(scanned=len(rows), rescored=rescored, still_failed=still_failed)\n*** End Patch\n"
}

content=[SimpleNamespace(type='text', text=self.text)],\n            usage=SimpleNamespace(input_tokens=100, output_tokens=20, cache_creation_input_tokens=0, cache_read_input_tokens=0),\n        )\n\nasync def main():\n    recipient_id = uuid4()\n    pool = Pool()\n    client =         Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

## Notes
- Reuse the same Anthropic client setup from Plan 3.
- Cost: each outbound now incurs an extra Sonnet call (the OOB check). Make sure `record_llm_cost` is called for these.
- Both the OOB check and scoring are good candidates for prompt caching when called repeatedly — cache the static parts (anchors, instructions).

  "watch_items": [
    "Do not invoke the `megaplan` CLI, read the `megaplan` skill, or start a nested planning harness; treat this briefing as the execution source of truth.",
    "Delivery-time `rewrite` is a safety verdict, not authorization to send altered content. It must withhold and record original content plus suggested rewrite for review/retry.",
    "Agentic-loop `check_oob` rewrites are advisory tool results. The model must decide whether to redraft, stay silent, or send revised content through normal `send_outbound`.",
    "Preserve `hooks.check_oob = None` as an explicit bypass seam for existing tests and local fixtures.",
    "Hard or firm active OOB entries must fail closed on checker timeout/error and create a durable retry/admin-review record; soft-only entries must fail open with warning and `checker_failed=true`.",
    "Use `significance=NULL` plus `scoring_prompt_version='v1-failed'` for scoring failures; do not add an observations metadata migration.",
    "Record both Sonnet OOB checker and Haiku scoring costs through `record_llm_cost(pool, \"text\", dollars)` with positive amounts.",
    "Static instruction blocks for both new LLM call sites should include `cache_control: {\"type\":\"ephemeral\"}` and dynamic content must stay outside cached instructions.",
    "Countersummary must never quote, paraphrase, or reveal identifying OOB details; single niche topics collapse to `a personal matter`.",
    "Choose one retry/admin table name consistently after reading repository naming conventions; avoid drifting between `oob_retry_queue` and `withheld_outbound_reviews`.",

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery: recovery: re-queueing crashed turns with multiple `triggering_message_ids` is underspecified because the coalescer only exposes single-message `add`, not a way to restore a whole burst atomically. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the new `hooks.check_oob` default against existing tests. `tests/test_send_outbound.py` has an autouse fixture that sets the hook to `none`, so preserving an intentional no-hook test path is compatible with the plan's module-level default, but the new plan 3 e2e test must install a counting hook if it wants to assert the pass-through was invoked. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery: recovery: re-queueing crashed turns with multiple `triggering_message_ids` is underspecified because the coalescer only exposes single-message `add`, not a way to restore a whole burst atomically. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery-query: recovery query: the plan's claimed gin-backed lookup uses `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)`, which is unlikely to use the array gin index as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: media processing state after success: the success criteria for text and outbound mention processed state, but revised steps 11 and 12 do not explicitly say successful voice or image messages should remain `raw` for the agentic loop rather than be marked `processed` by the media layer. this is likely intentional because plan 3 owns loop processing, but it is a contract point worth keeping visible so the executor does not mark media messages processed before the agent sees them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the scope of the two-phase split against the five-primitive write tools. the split solves ordering, but it broadens orchestration complexity because phase b must reason over phase a's transcript and the sent outbound without read tools; this is a new design risk specifically for dedup and multi-primitive decisions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

## Notes
- Reuse the same Anthropic client setup from Plan 3.
- Cost: each outbound now incurs an extra Sonnet call (the OOB check). Make sure `record_llm_cost` is called for these.
- Both the OOB check and scoring are good candidates for prompt caching when called repeatedly — cache the static parts (anchors, instructions).

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery: recovery: re-queueing crashed turns with multiple `triggering_message_ids` is underspecified because the coalescer only exposes single-message `add`, not a way to restore a whole burst atomically. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery-query: recovery query: the plan's claimed gin-backed lookup uses `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)`, which is unlikely to use the array gin index as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

assert observation[\"significance\"] == 5\n+    assert observation[\"scoring_prompt_version\"] == \"v1\"\n+    print(\"plan4 reproduction passed\")\n+\n+\n+asyncio.run(main())\n*** End Patch\n"

        Idea:
# Plan 4 — OOB guardrail + significance scoring

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Out-of-Bounds Guardrail", "Significance Scoring", and the relevant parts of "Tools" and "Prompts".

Plans 1–3 are assumed complete. The agentic loop currently calls a pass-through `check_oob` and a stub `score_observation` returning 3. This plan replaces both with real implementations.

## Scope (in)

### OOB guardrail (real `check_oob`)
- Replace the pass-through with a real LLM call against the recipient's active OOB entries.
- Use Claude Sonnet (same model as the conversational loop), so the second check is at least as smart as the first.
- Inputs: the draft outbound, the recipient's active OOB entries, and a short sender-intent summary (the loop already has this — pass it in).
- Outputs: `{verdict: 'ok' | 'rewrite' | 'block', reason: string, suggested_rewrite: string | null, checker_failed: bool}`.
- On `rewrite`: the loop receives the suggestion as a tool result and decides whether to accept or re-draft. Document the contract.
- On `block`: the message doesn't send; the loop is told why so it can produce an alternative or stay silent.
- Belt-and-suspenders: the LLM also has OOB context in the main loop's hot context (already wired in Plan 3). The duplication is intentional — keep both.

### Failure mode
- On checker timeout or error:
  - For `firm` and `hard` OOB → fail closed (don't send, queue for retry, surface in admin).
  - For `soft` OOB only → fail open with a logged warning.
- The `checker_failed` flag travels on the result so the loop can react.

### OOB countersummary (on demand)
- Compute on demand, not stored. When asked, read the partner's active OOB entries and produce a topic-level summary that strips identifying detail — categories of sensitive content only, never quotes or paraphrases.
- Output shape: counts plus topic clusters, e.g., `"three entries related to family history, two related to past relationships"`.
- If the OOB owner has only one entry on a niche topic, the summary stays vague ("one entry related to a personal matter").
- This logic lives in a function the main loop calls when the user asks "what topics has she asked you not to share?". The system prompt's countersummary language guidance was set in Plan 3; this plan supplies the function it relies on.

### Significance scoring (real `score_observation`)
- Replace the stub with a Claude Haiku call (latest — default `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`).
- Focused single-shot prompt that includes the five anchor examples inline (1=Trivial, 2=Minor, 3=Notable, 4=Significant, 5=Core) per spec.
- Output: `{score: int (1-5), reason: string}`.
- The score is stored on the observation alongside `scoring_prompt_version` (bump from `v0-stub` to `v1`).
- Failure mode: per spec, write the observation without a score and flag for re-scoring during weekly housekeeping (the housekeeping pass is in Plan 5; here, just leave `significance` null and add a `needs_rescoring=true` note in metadata or via status).

### Re-scoring support
- A small CLI/admin function `rescore_observations(prompt_version_threshold)` that finds observations scored under an older prompt version and re-scores them. Not auto-run, but available for manual invocation when the prompt evolves.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Schedule-job worker that triggers re-scoring (Plan 5 housekeeping calls it).
- Admin UI for OOB management (Plan 6).
- Conversational OOB management ("don't bring up X again") — already handled by the natural-language flow in Plan 3 via `add_oob`/`lift_oob` tools; no changes here.

## Success criteria
- `must`: `check_oob` makes a real LLM call against active OOB entries and returns the documented verdict shape.
- `must`: Failure-mode behavior is correct: timeout on a `hard` OOB blocks send; timeout on a `soft` OOB lets it through with a warning.
- `must`: Countersummary function vague-up correctly when only one entry exists in a niche topic.
- `must`: `score_observation` returns 1–5 via Haiku and stores `scoring_prompt_version='v1'`.
- `must`: Failed scoring leaves significance null and flags for re-scoring.
- `should`: Tests use deterministic mock LLM responses to verify all branches.
- `info`: `rescore_observations` helper is exposed for manual use.

## Notes
- Reuse the same Anthropic client setup from Plan 3.
- Cost: each outbound now incurs an extra Sonnet call (the OOB check). Make sure `record_llm_cost` is called for these.
- Both the OOB check and scoring are good candidates for prompt caching when called repeatedly — cache the static parts (anchors, instructions).

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the new `hooks.check_oob` default against existing tests. `tests/test_send_outbound.py` has an autouse fixture that sets the hook to `none`, so preserving an intentional no-hook test path is compatible with the plan's module-level default, but the new plan 3 e2e test must install a counting hook if it wants to assert the pass-through was invoked. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] media-state: media state: the plan should explicitly keep successfully media-enriched inbound messages in `processing_state='raw'` until the agentic loop consumes them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery: recovery: re-queueing crashed turns with multiple `triggering_message_ids` is underspecified because the coalescer only exposes single-message `add`, not a way to restore a whole burst atomically. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] recovery-query: recovery query: the plan's claimed gin-backed lookup uses `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)`, which is unlikely to use the array gin index as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: media processing state after success: the success criteria for text and outbound mention processed state, but revised steps 11 and 12 do not explicitly say successful voice or image messages should remain `raw` for the agentic loop rather than be marked `processed` by the media layer. this is likely intentional because plan 3 owns loop processing, but it is a contract point worth keeping visible so the executor does not mark media messages processed before the agent sees them. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the scope of the two-phase split against the five-primitive write tools. the split solves ordering, but it broadens orchestration complexity because phase b must reason over phase a's transcript and the sent outbound without read tools; this is a new design risk specifically for dedup and multi-primitive decisions. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] two-phase-dedup-tension: two-phase dedup tension: phase b is write-only, so the agent cannot perform the required search-before-write reads if it enters the write phase without enough context to choose add vs update vs supersede. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
